- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/Mcp/McpToolkit.php` — the class docblock carries the argument, then the code:
> One remote MCP server as a toolkit: the tools an administrator approved, still hashing to the fingerprint they approved, named `{prefix}__{name}` and called through the client.
>
> The tool list is fetched once per instance and memoised: `tools()` is asked by the agent for every provider call and by the system prompt, and each ask is a network round trip otherwise. It is fetched per *turn* rather than cached across requests on purpose — the pin is checked against the live definition, which is what makes a redefinition take effect at once rather than a cache's lifetime later. That is one `tools/list` per server per turn, which is the price of the guarantee; a site that adds a server it does not want called pays it on every turn, which is why a server with nothing approved is never built at all (Mcp\Toolkits).
>
> Three kinds of tool are not offered: one that was never approved, one whose live fingerprint differs from the pinned one (withheld, and reported through `$onDrift` so the settings row can say so), and every tool of a server that could not be listed — a server that is down makes a turn with no remote tools, never a failed turn. `hash_equals()` compares the pins because it is a comparison of two hashes and there is no reason to write the variable-time one.
>
> A call is named back: the model calls `trk__search`, the server is asked for `search`. The names are ToolName::fit()'s, so a server's 100-character tool name still reaches every provider.
>
> Every call fires `alpaca_bot/mcp/called` with its arguments, the acting user and the result — the specification asks a client to log tool calls, and a site that wants an audit trail hooks that. The plugin's own record of what ran is the reply's `meta.tool_calls`, which the observer writes for every tool alike; a receipt never carries arguments, because a receipt is numbers (Chat\UsageMeter's docblock), and arguments are content.
>
> What the server returns is the model's input, and what its description says is the model's reading: the guidelines say so in the system prompt, which is the only place that can say it. A failed call answers a fixed sentence naming the prefix, never the exception's message, for SummarizeToolkit::tool()'s reason — a tool error is text the model may repeat, and a transport's message quotes the endpoint.

```php
public function tools(): array
{
    if ($this->tools !== null) {
        return $this->tools;
    }
    $this->tools = [];
    if ($this->server->approved === []) {
        return $this->tools;
    }
    try {
        $definitions = $this->client->listTools();
    } catch (\RuntimeException) {
        return $this->tools;
    }
    $drifted = [];
    foreach ($definitions as $definition) {
        $pinned = $this->server->approved[$definition->name] ?? null;
        if ($pinned === null) {
            continue;
        }
        if (!hash_equals($pinned, $definition->fingerprint())) {
            $drifted[] = $definition->name;
            continue;
        }
        $name = $definition->name;
        $this->tools[] = new SchemaTool(
            ToolName::fit($this->server->prefix . '__' . $name),
            SchemaTool::describe($definition->description),
            $definition->inputSchema,
            fn(array $arguments): ToolResult => $this->call($name, $arguments),
        );
    }
    if ($this->onDrift !== null) {
        ($this->onDrift)($this->server->id, $drifted);
    }
    return $this->tools;
}

private function call(string $name, array $arguments): ToolResult
{
    try {
        $result = $this->client->callTool($name, $arguments);
    } catch (\RuntimeException) {
        /* translators: %s: the MCP server's prefix, e.g. trk */
        $result = ToolResult::error(sprintf(__('The %s server did not answer the call.', 'alpaca-bot'), $this->server->prefix));
    }
    /**
     * Fires after a remote MCP tool has been called, whether it answered or not. The one hook
     * for an audit trail of what the model asked another party to do on this site's behalf: the
     * arguments are the model's, the user is whose turn it was, and the ToolResult carries the
     * server's answer (or the plugin's refusal when the server did not answer). It fires
     * in-process and stores nothing.
     *
     * @since 0.6.0
     * @param string               $serverId  the server's id, as its Access row `mcp.<id>` names it
     * @param string               $tool      the tool's name on the server, not the prefixed name the model used
     * @param array<string, mixed> $arguments what the model sent
     * @param int                  $userId    the user whose turn it is
     * @param ToolResult           $result    what came back
     */
    do_action('alpaca_bot/mcp/called', $this->server->id, $name, $arguments, ($this->userId)(), $result);
    return $result;
}
```

`src/Mcp/Toolkits.php` — walks `toolkits.mcp_servers`, skips a row with no id or nothing approved, asks `Access::allows($userId, 'mcp.' . $id)` and skips a user who fails it (so nothing is built and no credential is read for a user who may not use the server), merges the header value through `Secrets::resolve()`, and returns `['mcp.' . $id => new McpToolkit($this->clients->for($server), $server, $this->userId, Drift::set(...))]`. Its docblock notes that the Access row's default is `manage_options` and is not listed in `Access::defaults()` because the rows are per server; and that the key is `mcp.<id>` so that what `alpaca_bot/toolkits` receives names the server the way every other surface does.

`src/Toolkit/Registry.php` — the constructor takes `?Mcp\Toolkits $mcp = null` after Task 8's `Access`, and `enabled()` adds them after the floor and before the filter:
```php
$subset += $this->mcp?->for($userId) ?? [];
```
The "Two gates, in order" paragraph becomes three sources, in order, and says what each is: the `toolkits.enabled` setting over the built-ins, then the tool floor (Task 8), then the MCP servers — which are **not** in `toolkits.enabled`, because their ids are not known to the schema, and are gated by their own `mcp.<id>` row instead; and only then `alpaca_bot/toolkits`, which still sees and can remove everything. The hook's own docblock (:68-87) is rewritten in the same edit: its "This filter is also, today, the only capability gate over the tools" sentence is already Task 8's to rewrite, and this task checks it now names the floor **and** the MCP rows.

`src/Chat/AgentStreamObserver.php` — `answer()` adds `'result_bytes' => strlen($result->content)` to the record and `toolCalls()` adds `'result_bytes' => 0` to an unanswered call; the class docblock's "A tool call record is `{name, arguments, result_excerpt, ok}`" becomes `{name, arguments, result_excerpt, ok, result_bytes}` with a sentence saying what the new field is for: the excerpt is bounded for storage, so the size of what actually came back would otherwise be unknowable, and that size is what the usage receipt reports — a remote tool can return a megabyte that costs context and money without spending a completion token.

`src/Chat/Pipeline.php` — both receipt sites sum it: `array_sum(array_column($toolCalls, 'result_bytes'))`, passed to `UsageMeter::record()` and added to the `Result`'s receipt as `tool_result_bytes`. The four docblock shapes listing the record's keys are updated with it.

`src/Chat/UsageMeter.php` — `record()` takes `int $toolResultBytes = 0` last, stores `max(0, …)` in `meta_input['tool_result_bytes']` and puts it in the receipt; the `alpaca_bot/usage/recorded` docblock's key list gains it, and the "numbers only" sentence stays true and is worth re-reading: this is a number, it is stored whatever `privacy.usage_log` says, exactly as the token counts are, and it carries nothing about *what* a tool returned.

`docs/api.md` — the receipt's key list and the two sample bodies gain `tool_result_bytes`; a sentence says it is the total size in bytes of every tool result the turn received, 0 for a turn that ran none. `docs/hooks.md` is regenerated for `alpaca_bot/mcp/called`. `HelpTabs::tools()` and README's Tools section gain the MCP paragraph: what adding a server grants, that tool descriptions and results are the remote server's text, that approval pins a definition and a changed one is withheld until it is approved again, that a server is administrators-only until its row under Settings › Access says otherwise, and that every call is checked against the same address rules web_fetch uses.

### Task 28: Swap in php-agents 0.16 (BLOCKED on php-agents `v0.16.0`)

**Blocked.** Nothing here starts until `carmelosantana/php-agents` `v0.16.0` is tagged on GitHub and its **frozen contract is posted as a comment on Kanboard #4364** (the php-agents wayfinding session owns both — Kanboard project 79). Check with `gh api repos/carmelosantana/php-agents/releases --jq '.[].tag_name'` before picking this up; if the tag is not there, the task stays blocked and 0.6.0 does not ship (Task 30's first precondition).

**The frozen contract (2026-09-17).** The php-agents session froze it and posted it verbatim on Kanboard #4364 (comment 1067; decision on Kanboard #4404, project 79). Read that comment before starting; this is what the task depends on. Namespace `CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp`, which Strauss makes `AlpacaBot\Vendor\CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp`.
- `McpServer(string $url, array $headers = [], float $timeout = 30.0, int $maxResponseBytes = 1_048_576)`. **There is no `prefix`** — it moved to the toolkit. Later decisions may only add trailing optional parameters, so **always construct it with named arguments**.
- `McpClientInterface { listTools(): list<McpToolDefinition>; callTool(string $name, array $arguments): ToolResult }`, implemented by `McpClient(McpServer $server, Symfony\Contracts\HttpClient\HttpClientInterface $http, ?McpSessionStore $sessions = null)`. The client talks to `$server->url` only, never follows a redirect, and enforces `maxResponseBytes` and `timeout` itself as well as through Task 14's wrapper. `listTools()` follows `nextCursor` to the end.
- `McpToolDefinition(string $name, string $description, array $inputSchema, array $annotations = [], ?string $title = null)`. Its `fingerprint()` is **byte-identical** to Task 24's `Mcp\ToolDefinition::fingerprint()`: the same four JSON flags, `ksort(SORT_STRING)` on every non-list array, lists never re-ordered, title excluded, computed over `json_decode(..., true)` values. Verified against this plan's Task 24 code on 2026-09-17. Annotations are hashed **as sent**, `annotations.title` included, so the adapter must pass them through untouched.
- Errors all extend `\RuntimeException` through `McpException`: `McpTransportException` (with `McpRedirectException` for any 3xx), `McpAuthException` (401/403), and `McpProtocolException` (with `McpRpcException` and `McpUnsupportedVersionException`).
- php-agents also ships `McpToolkit(McpClientInterface $client, array $allow, string $prefix = '', ?\Closure $onDrift = null, ?\Closure $namer = null)` and `McpToolName::fit()`, whose default naming is the same rule as `Toolkit\ToolName::fit()`. **This task does not adopt them.** The plugin's own `Mcp\McpToolkit` (Task 27) stays: it is already built and tested against the plugin's seam, its drift reporting feeds the settings screen, and swapping toolkits at the last task would be churn for no behaviour change. A later release can switch if there's a reason to.
- **Still open, additive only:** the `McpSessionStore` shape (Kanboard #4405), protocol-era negotiation (Kanboard #4406), result mapping (Kanboard #4409) and annotation surfacing (Kanboard #4410). Re-read #4364 before starting: if any of them changed the surface above, the php-agents session will have posted again there.

**The rule if something still differs:** this task adapts the seam, not the plugin. `Mcp\ClientInterface`, `ServerConfig`, `ToolDefinition`, `Drift`, `McpToolkit` and every caller of them stay exactly as Tasks 24-27 left them, and the whole of any difference lands inside `Mcp\PhpAgentsClient` and the session store. If the contract cannot be adapted behind those two classes, stop and take it back to Kanboard #4364 rather than reshaping the plugin's half.

Expected: `composer.json` and `composer.lock` move and **nothing else** — `vendor/` and `vendor-prefixed/` are git-ignored (`.gitignore:3` and `:7`), so the prefixed tree is rebuilt on every checkout by `composer install`'s `post-install-cmd` and is never committed. The grep must show the new MCP classes under the `AlpacaBot\Vendor\` prefix; if they are unprefixed, the Strauss `packages` list or the release's namespace changed, and that is a stop-and-ask.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the adapter**
`src/Mcp/PhpAgentsClient.php` — thin, and its docblock says why it is thin: the seam exists so the plugin's half could be built before the library, and now that the library is here the adapter's whole job is to translate two things and nothing else — the library's tool definition into `Mcp\ToolDefinition` (so the fingerprints an administrator approved keep matching, because the pin is the plugin's canonicalisation and must not become the library's), and the library's failures into `Mcp\McpUnavailable` (so `Mcp\McpToolkit`'s `catch (\RuntimeException)` keeps meaning "no tools from here" whatever the library throws). Everything else — the protocol era, the session if there is one, content blocks, retries — is the library's, which is the point of having asked it for a client. `over()` builds the library's objects from a `ServerConfig`, exactly as the frozen contract shapes them:

```php
use AlpacaBot\Vendor\CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp\McpClient;
use AlpacaBot\Vendor\CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp\McpClientInterface;
use AlpacaBot\Vendor\CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp\McpServer;
use AlpacaBot\Vendor\CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp\McpSessionStore;
use AlpacaBot\Vendor\CarmeloSantana\PHPAgents\Mcp\McpToolDefinition;

public static function over(ServerConfig $server, Egress $egress, ?McpSessionStore $sessions = null): self
{
    // Named arguments on purpose: the contract only ever adds trailing optional parameters.
    $library = new McpServer(
        url: $server->url,
        headers: ($server->headerName !== '' && $server->headerValue !== '')
            ? [$server->headerName => $server->headerValue]
            : [],
        timeout: $server->timeout,
        maxResponseBytes: $server->maxBytes,
    );
    return new self(new McpClient($library, $egress->client($server), $sessions));
}

public function __construct(private McpClientInterface $client) {}

/** @return list<ToolDefinition> */
public function listTools(): array
{
    try {
        return array_map(
            // Annotations pass through untouched: annotations.title is part of the pinned hash.
            static fn(McpToolDefinition $d): ToolDefinition => new ToolDefinition($d->name, $d->description, $d->inputSchema, $d->annotations, $d->title),
            $this->client->listTools(),
        );
    } catch (\RuntimeException $e) {
        throw new McpUnavailable($e->getMessage(), 0, $e);
    }
}
```

`callTool()` is the same shape: it returns the library's `ToolResult` unchanged and maps a `\RuntimeException` to `McpUnavailable`. Test the header rule explicitly — an empty name or an empty value sends no header — and that the header value never appears in an exception message. The header value `ServerConfig` carries is the one Task 25's `Mcp\Secrets` resolves; check that `fromSettings()` hands `over()` the real value, not the mask.

Unit-test the adapter over the library's own `McpClientInterface` with a small in-test fake, and one case over the real `McpClient` with the prefixed `MockHttpClient` (Step 2). Assert in that case that a fingerprint computed by `McpToolDefinition` equals the one `ToolDefinition` computes for the same definition: that equality is what keeps approved pins valid, and it's cheap to pin.
`src/Mcp/ClientFactory.php` — the default closure becomes `static fn(ServerConfig $server): ClientInterface => PhpAgentsClient::over($server, $egress)`, with the `Mcp\Egress` handed to the factory's constructor (`__construct(?\Closure $build = null, ?Egress $egress = null)`, defaulting to `new Egress()`), and `src/Plugin.php` builds it that way. `src/Mcp/UnavailableClient.php` and `McpUnavailable::NOT_YET` are deleted; the docblock sentences in `ClientFactory`, `ClientInterface` and `Mcp\Discovery` that say "until php-agents 0.16 is bundled" are rewritten to describe what is there now, and so is every user-facing sentence that said the client had not arrived (README's Tools section, the Tools help tab, `docs/api.md`'s note on the discovery route).
**The session store.** The frozen contract has one: `McpClient` takes `?McpSessionStore $sessions = null`, but the interface's shape is still open on Kanboard #4405. Read what #4405 decided (it will be posted on #4364), then implement `src/Mcp/TransientSessions.php` against that interface, backed by `get_transient`/`set_transient` under `alpaca_bot_mcp_session_{id}` with an hour's expiry, keyed by server id and not by user (the session belongs to the credential the site stores, not to the person chatting), and pass it through `over()`. If #4405 is still undecided when the tag exists, pass `null` — the client works without a store at the cost of a handshake per turn against 2025-11-25 servers — and say so in the task report.

- [ ] **Step 5: The integration test against a real server**
`tests/Integration/McpClientLiveTest.php` is **opt-in and skips with its reason**: it runs only when `ALPACA_BOT_MCP_URL`, `ALPACA_BOT_MCP_HEADER` and `ALPACA_BOT_MCP_IP` are set, and otherwise `markTestSkipped('No MCP server configured; set ALPACA_BOT_MCP_URL, ALPACA_BOT_MCP_HEADER and ALPACA_BOT_MCP_IP to run this against one.')` — Task 2 made the suite hermetic, and a test that reached the network by default would undo that. It asserts one round trip end to end: `tools/list` answers at least one definition, its fingerprint is stable across two calls, and a read-only tool call returns a result.
The server to point it at is the **WordPress MCP Adapter plugin** (`v0.6.1`, a plugin, not a library — bundling it is deprecated by its PR #288) on a throwaway harness site: create it, install and activate the adapter, register a custom server through `mcp_adapter_init` exposing one read-only ability, and authenticate with an application password in the static header. Two things make that awkward and the test states both: the site is `*.wp.test`, which resolves to loopback and `Toolkit\AddressPin` refuses, so the test builds its own `Mcp\Egress` with the injected resolver (`new Egress(static fn(string $host): string => getenv('ALPACA_BOT_MCP_IP'))`) — the one place in the suite that goes round the address check, done in a test's own object and never in shipped code; and the harness's certificate must be trusted inside whichever container runs the suite, failing which the test skips with that reason rather than being disabled. If neither can be arranged on this machine, the task reports "skipped, and why" and the manual equivalent is run once from the host with a short script, with its output pasted into the report — the release does not depend on a green CI run of this test, but it does depend on somebody having seen the client talk to a real server.

- [ ] **Step 6: Run the gates**
Run: `composer check`, `WPH_SITE=alpaca10 bash bin/test-integration.sh`, `pnpm check`, `bash bin/build-zip.sh` (the zip now carries the new `vendor-prefixed/` tree and its content assertions must still pass), and `composer docs:readme && git diff --exit-code README.md readme.txt`.

- [ ] **Step 7: Commit**
```bash
git add composer.json composer.lock src/Mcp/PhpAgentsClient.php src/Mcp/ClientFactory.php src/Mcp/McpUnavailable.php src/Mcp/Discovery.php src/Plugin.php src/Admin/HelpTabs.php README.md readme.txt docs/api.md tests/Unit/Mcp/PhpAgentsClientTest.php tests/Unit/Mcp/ClientFactoryTest.php tests/Integration/McpClientLiveTest.php
git rm src/Mcp/UnavailableClient.php
git commit -m "build(deps): php-agents ~0.16.0, and the real mcp client behind the seam"
```

**Sentences re-checked:** every "until php-agents 0.16" / "arrives with php-agents 0.16" sentence in `src/Mcp/*`, the help tab, the README and `docs/api.md` — this task is the one that falsifies all of them, and the grep for them is part of Step 4 (`grep -rn "php-agents 0.16" src README.md readme.txt docs` must come back empty afterwards).

## Phase G: Release

### Task 29: Docs, readme, changelog and upgrade notice for 0.6.0

**Files:**
- Modify: `README.md` (§ Features, § Settings, § Changelog — a new `### 0.6.0` above `### 0.5.0`), `readme.txt` (`== Upgrade Notice ==`, hand-written; its `== Changelog ==` and `== Description ==` come from README through `composer docs:readme`), `docs/api.md` (the `GET /settings` sample and its "Three of the 24 fields" count), `docs/hooks.md` (regenerated), `alpaca-bot.php` (:24), `src/Settings/Schema.php` (:99, the fetch user agent), `src/Admin/HelpTabs.php` (whatever the sweep finds)
- Test: `tests/Unit/ReadmeSyncTest.php` and `tests/Unit/HooksDocTest.php` are the existing no-diff gates and must stay green; no new test file.

**Interfaces:** consumes nothing, produces nothing. It is the release's paperwork, and its gate is that two greps come back empty and two generators produce no diff.

- [ ] **Step 1: Run the sweep, and watch it fail**
```bash
# (a) sentences 0.6 falsifies. Each hit is either already rewritten by the task that owned it,
#     or a straggler this task fixes.
grep -rn -i "later 0\.x\|a 0\.6\|until 0\.6\|for 0\.6\|0\.6 work\|0\.6 ticket\|0\.6 separates\|php-agents 0\.16" \
  src docs/api.md docs/hooks.md bin/hooks-doc.php README.md readme.txt resources tests alpaca-bot.php
# (b) the version this release makes wrong in shipped strings
grep -rn "AlpacaBot/0\.5\|Alpaca Bot 0\.5" src alpaca-bot.php docs/api.md README.md readme.txt
```
Expected before the task: hits. The ones that must be **gone** (each owned by an earlier task; if one survives, fix it here and say so in the report): `src/Toolkit/Registry.php`'s "A floor of enabled()'s own is a later 0.x release" and `docs/hooks.md`'s copy of it plus the hand-written preamble that repeats it (`bin/hooks-doc.php:526` onward, "Before you loosen a capability filter" — it is generated *from the generator*, so the sentence lives in the PHP, not the Markdown); `src/Admin/HelpTabs.php:140` ("Pinning the resolved address into the connection is a later 0.x release") and `:147-153` ("there is no second check between a role that may chat and the tools that are on"); `src/Admin/HelpTabs.php:146` and README/readme's "An egress policy is the supported mitigation"; `README.md:104`/`readme.txt:59` (the same pinning sentence); `README.md:110`/`readme.txt:65` ("Until a later 0.x release adds a floor of its own"); `README.md:148`/`readme.txt:99` ("A per-site capability setting for this belongs to the admin-wide panel of a later 0.x release"); `src/Rest/SettingsController.php:34` and `:75` ("a 0.6 ticket", "until 0.6 separates the keys"); `src/Settings/Migrate04.php:304` ("left as it is for 0.5 and tracked for 0.6"); `tests/e2e/composer.spec.ts:22` ("unit-testable is 0.6 work"); every "php-agents 0.16" sentence (Task 28).
The ones that must **stay**, because they are still true and this release does not falsify them — name them in the report so a reviewer knows they were read, not missed: `src/Shortcodes/AgentShim.php:128`, `src/Admin/HelpTabs.php:111`, `README.md:162`/`:229` and `readme.txt:111`/`:191` (`[alpacabot_agent]` still goes away in a later 0.x release); `src/Shortcodes/Chat.php:28` and `README.md:158`/`readme.txt:107` (a front-end chat design of its own is 0.7).
And two this task owns outright: `alpaca-bot.php:24` says "Alpaca Bot 0.5 requires PHP 8.4 or newer" in the notice a site below the floor sees — it becomes version-free ("Alpaca Bot requires PHP 8.4 or newer. The plugin is inactive."), because that string is not a version marker and having one there means every release has to remember it; and `src/Settings/Schema.php:99`'s default fetch user agent is the literal `AlpacaBot/0.5 (+…)`, which this release makes wrong for every new site — it becomes `'AlpacaBot/' . Plugin::VERSION . ' (+https://github.com/carmelosantana/alpaca-bot)'`, so the release bump carries it, with a sentence in the field's description saying a site that has saved its settings keeps the value it stored (`Schema::sanitize()` keeps a stored value, which is the whole point of that rule) and can clear the field to take the new default. `docs/api.md`'s sample of the same string moves with it.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the changelog and the upgrade notice**
`README.md` gains `### 0.6.0` above `### 0.5.0`, in the same shape the 0.5.0 entry uses — a one-line lead, then `<details><summary>…</summary>` folds, which `bin/readme-sync.php` renders back to `= 0.6.0 =` and bold headings in `readme.txt` (its docblock explains the fold conversion, and `ReadmeSyncTest` pins that the header block above the first `== … ==` line is never touched). The folds, in this order, because the first two are what an upgrading site feels:

**Breaking**
- **A tool now needs a capability of its own, not just the chat one.** `Toolkit\Registry::enabled()` drops any toolkit whose Access row the user fails before `alpaca_bot/toolkits` runs. A default site sees no change: the built-in rows are the capabilities those surfaces already asked for. A site that opened the chat to a wider role keeps chat for them and loses the tools until an administrator lowers the row under **Settings › Access** (Kanboard #4331).
- **The settings capability filter is two filters.** `GET /settings` and `/settings/schema` ask `alpaca_bot/capability/settings/read`, `PUT /settings` asks `…/write`, and both default to whatever the old `alpaca_bot/capability/settings` returns, so an existing filter keeps working and can now be split. `?reveal=1` still asks `manage_options` outright (Kanboard #4332).
- **A stream refused because you already have one open answers its own code**, `alpaca_bot_stream_concurrency`, where it used to answer `alpaca_bot_rate_limited`; the per-minute bucket keeps the old code. A client that matched on the old code for both now tells them apart — which is the point (Kanboard #4333).
- **web_fetch refuses a fetch WordPress would not make with cURL**, and pins the address it checked into the connection. A site whose HTTP transport is not cURL loses the tool rather than making an unpinned request, and Site Health says so (Kanboard #4330).
- **`Rest\Errors` and `Rest\RateLimit` are now `AlpacaBot\Errors` and `AlpacaBot\RateLimit`.** Site code that referenced them by their old names has to move; the REST routes, the hooks and the wire are unchanged.
- **Every usage receipt carries `tool_result_bytes`.** A client that validated the receipt's shape strictly will see the new key.

**Added** — the chat drawer on every admin screen, with the screen's own context as removable chips; a native sidebar in the block editor; **Settings › Access**, one capability per surface, with "set in code" where a filter decides it; the abilities toolkit, an allowlist of this site's WordPress abilities the model may call as the chatting user; remote MCP servers, per-tool approval pinned to the definition approved, with a changed definition withheld until it is reviewed; the pinned egress both the fetch tool and the MCP client go through.

**Fixed** — "Provider error:" only where the provider actually failed (Kanboard #4327); the 0.4 conversation migration is bounded per row and can no longer retry for ever (Kanboard #4335); a faked tool call no longer flickers through the reply while it streams (Kanboard #4329); the per-model overrides table stays inside the page with the admin menu expanded; the integration suite reaches no network (Kanboard #4322).

`readme.txt`'s `== Upgrade Notice ==` gains, above 0.5.0 and hand-written (the sync never touches that section):
> = 0.6.0 =
> Tools now need a capability of their own as well as chat: a site that opened chat to a wider role keeps chat and loses the tools until an administrator sets the rows under Settings › Access. The settings capability filter splits into read and write keys; the old key still sets both. New: a chat drawer on every admin screen, WordPress abilities as tools, and remote MCP servers.

### Task 30: The 0.6.0 release commit, the gates, Plugin Check, and a tag only on Carmelo's word

**Files:**
- Modify, in one commit: `alpaca-bot.php` (:6 `Version:`), `package.json` (:3 `"version"`), `src/Plugin.php` (:12 `VERSION`), `readme.txt` (`Stable tag:`)
- Touch nothing else. The other three `readme.txt` header rows — `Requires PHP: 8.4`, `Requires at least: 6.9`, `Tested up to: 7.1` — stay where they are: 0.6 moves no floor, and the repo `CLAUDE.md` rule is that those four rows are one decision that moves together or not at all (wordpress.org holds one `Requires PHP` and one `Requires at least` per *plugin*, not per release). `bin/version-check.sh` compares the two support rows between `readme.txt` and the plugin header rather than against the version, and only for a release version, which is exactly this case.

**Preconditions, checked and reported before anything is edited:**
1. Tasks 1-29 are merged on `develop` and green. `git log --oneline main..develop` shows them, and `git log --oneline develop..main` is empty.
2. **Task 28 is done.** 0.6.0 ships an MCP client that works; shipping the settings, the approval UI and a client that refuses would put a half-feature on wordpress.org. If php-agents `v0.16.0` still is not tagged when everything else is ready, this is a decision for Carmelo, not for the plan: ask him, in those words, whether to wait or to cut 0.6.0 without §6 (which means reverting Tasks 24-27's user-facing surface, not just leaving it inert).
3. `bash bin/build-zip.sh` has been run at least once on this tree and `dist/alpaca-bot.zip` builds.

- [ ] **Step 1: The release commit**
```bash
git switch develop && git pull --ff-only
# the four markers, one commit:
#   alpaca-bot.php:6   Version: 0.6.0
#   package.json:3     "version": "0.6.0"
#   src/Plugin.php:12  public const VERSION = '0.6.0';
#   readme.txt         Stable tag: 0.6.0
bash bin/version-check.sh 0.6.0
```
Expected: `versions ok: 0.6.0`, and no `MISMATCH` or `SKIP` line (a `SKIP` means a support header was renamed or dropped and the two files were never compared — treat it as a failure).
```bash
git add alpaca-bot.php package.json src/Plugin.php readme.txt
git commit -m "chore(release): 0.6.0"
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Every gate, with its tail in the report**
```bash
composer check
WPH_SITE=alpaca10 bash bin/test-integration.sh
pnpm check
pnpm e2e
composer docs:hooks && git diff --exit-code docs/hooks.md
composer docs:readme && git diff --exit-code README.md readme.txt
bash bin/build-zip.sh
bash bin/version-check.sh 0.6.0
```
All green, and `bin/build-zip.sh`'s own content assertions (every runtime path present, nothing from the dev toolchain, no dotfiles) pass — it runs `version-check.sh` first and restores the dev dependencies through its EXIT trap, so `composer check` still works in the tree afterwards.

- [ ] **Step 3: Plugin Check against the built zip, the 0.5 way**
Never against the mounted checkout — the repo tree and the shipped tree are different plugins (`plugin-check.yml`'s own preamble says why), and a wordpress.org reviewer downloads the zip.
```bash
mkdir -p ~/"Projects/Alpaca Bot/releases/0.6.0" && cd ~/"Projects/Alpaca Bot/releases/0.6.0"
cp <worktree>/dist/alpaca-bot.zip . && unzip -q -o alpaca-bot.zip && sha256sum alpaca-bot.zip > SHA256SUMS
# mount this unpacked build on the throwaway site 0.5 used (alpacapc), never alpaca10 and never alpacabot
node ~/Projects/wp-harness/bin/wph.js wp alpacapc -- plugin check alpaca-bot
```
Compare against the 0.5.0 baseline recorded for that site: **42 findings, of which 37 are `ExceptionNotEscaped`**, all judged false positives or justified. Report the new list as a diff by file and line, not as a count: a new finding in 0.6 code is the thing this step exists to catch. Fix anything error-level that is real; anything dismissed is dismissed *in the report*, with its reason.
Then the AI pass, and **reconcile its coverage**: `wp plugin check alpaca-bot --ai --ai-model=<provider::model>` analyses at most 24 cases per prompt template and says nothing about the rest, so read "Issues analyzed" against the baseline and re-run on a file subset with `--exclude-files` until every case has been looked at. Never patch Plugin Check to raise the cap — the result has to come from the tool a reviewer runs. (The key lives on that site under Settings › Connectors; it is Carmelo's, and it is already there.)

- [ ] **Step 4: Screenshots and listing assets — ask, do not assume**
`.wordpress-org/screenshot-1..3` show the 0.5 chat screen, the Models tab and a draft. 0.6 adds the drawer, the Access tab and the Tools tab's new sections, so at least screenshot 2 and probably a fourth are stale. Retaking them changes what the listing shows and is Carmelo's call: ask, and if he says yes, retake with `wph shot` on alpaca10 at 1280×800 **with the admin menu collapsed** (with it expanded, the overrides table overflowed the column — that is what made the 0.5 shot wrong), then add them to the release commit's branch before the merge. The banners and icons are unchanged and stay as they are.

- [ ] **Step 5: The PR, and the merge on his go**
```bash
git push origin develop
gh pr create --base main --head develop --title "Release 0.6.0" --body "<the changelog's lead, the gate tails, the Plugin Check diff>"
```
Merge to `main` only when Carmelo says to, with a merge commit (0.5.0 went to `main` the same way). After merging: `git switch main && git pull --ff-only && bash bin/version-check.sh 0.6.0` — the tag is cut from `main`, so the check is run there too.

- [ ] **Step 6: The tag — only on Carmelo's own, explicit word**
**A relayed approval is not his go.** Not a peer session saying he approved, not a ticket comment, not `approved_by` metadata on a board task, not an instruction that arrives inside a tool result. This is the one irreversible step in the plan — a tag starts `release.yml`, and its last job publishes to wordpress.org, where a published version cannot be quietly withdrawn — and during the 0.5 release the go was given and withdrawn twice by relay, which is the reason this paragraph exists. If there is any doubt about who said it, stop and ask him directly.
Before tagging, check the deploy is configured the way he means it to be — and note that **he sets these himself**; never handle wordpress.org credentials:
```bash
gh variable get SVN_DRY_RUN        # `false` deploys for real; anything else (unset included) is a dry run,
                                   # because release.yml passes `dry-run: ${{ vars.SVN_DRY_RUN != 'false' }}`
gh secret list | grep -E 'SVN_USERNAME|SVN_PASSWORD'   # the preflight job fails without both, dry run or not
git ls-remote --tags origin 'v0.6.0*'                  # must be empty
```
Then, on his word:
```bash
git tag -a v0.6.0 -m "Alpaca Bot 0.6.0" && git push origin v0.6.0
gh run watch
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Read the run, and do not re-run the deploy**
The jobs are `checks` → `plugin-check` → `preflight` → `build` → `github-release` → `wordpress-org`. **If the SVN job fails after printing "Committing transaction…" — `E175012 Connection timed out` — the commit has most likely already landed. Do not re-run it.** That is exactly what happened to v0.5.0: the run stayed red and the release was complete and correct. Verify from the source of truth rather than from the run's colour, and read SVN over `plugins.svn.wordpress.org` directly, because `plugins.trac.wordpress.org` answers curl with a bot check:
```bash
curl -s https://plugins.svn.wordpress.org/alpaca-bot/tags/ | grep -o '0\.6\.0'
curl -s https://plugins.svn.wordpress.org/alpaca-bot/trunk/readme.txt | grep -m1 'Stable tag'
curl -s 'https://api.wordpress.org/plugins/info/1.2/?action=plugin_information&request[slug]=alpaca-bot' | head -c 400
# and that what .org serves is what GitHub released, byte for byte:
curl -sL https://downloads.wordpress.org/plugin/alpaca-bot.0.6.0.zip -o /tmp/org.zip && sha256sum /tmp/org.zip
gh release download v0.6.0 -p alpaca-bot.zip -O /tmp/gh.zip && sha256sum /tmp/gh.zip
```
Only if SVN genuinely holds nothing for 0.6.0 is a re-run the right move.

- [ ] **Step 8: Afterwards**
Comment the release URL and the verification output on the Kanboard release ticket and close it (never set `approved_by`, and never run `kanboard-run-milestone.sh` — either arms a watcher that launches headless workers). Fast-forward local `main` and `develop`. Keep `~/Projects/Alpaca Bot/releases/0.6.0/` with its `SHA256SUMS` and the Plugin Check runs, as 0.5.0's is kept.

- [ ] **Step 9: Commit**
Nothing to commit here beyond Step 1's release commit and whatever Step 4 added: the tag and the merge are the artefacts.

## Merge points (read before starting a phase)

These are the places where two tasks touch the same thing. Each names the owner and what the other task does instead.

1. **`Mcp\ServerConfig` is created in Task 14**, because Task 14's `Egress::client(ServerConfig $server)` needs it and comes first. Task 24 **consumes** it and neither re-creates it nor re-tests it. `$approved` is the last constructor parameter with a default, since required-after-optional is deprecated in PHP 8.
2. **MCP Access rows need storage that Task 6 must provide.** `Access::stored('mcp.<id>')` reads `access.mcp.<id>`, but `Schema::sanitize()` rebuilds the option from `fields()` and drops anything it doesn't declare, so a per-server key can never be saved. **Task 6 adds one `access.mcp` map field** (server id => capability, values validated against `Access::CAPABILITIES`, unknown ids dropped), and `Access::stored()` reads a `mcp.` row out of that map. Tasks 21, 25, 26 and 27 use the row names unchanged (`mcp.<id>`); only the storage is a map. Until a server exists the map is empty and every MCP row reads `manage_options`, which fails closed.
3. **The drawer's image button (Carmelo, 2026-09-15): show it only where the media library is already loaded.** `Assets.php:119-124` explains why `wp_enqueue_media()` is not called unconditionally — it is the whole media library, and the picker is empty for a user without `upload_files`. So:
   - **Task 18:** `drawer.ts` hides `[data-action="image"]` when `window.wp.media` is absent, which is most admin screens. The drawer never calls `wp_enqueue_media()`.
   - **Task 20:** in the block editor the post screen already loads media, so the sidebar keeps the button. Verify that on the site (`wp.media` is defined on `post.php`) rather than assuming it; if it isn't, hide the button there too and say so.
   - The chat screen, the front-end shortcode and their rules are unchanged.
4. **The Chat row's "set in code" is per surface, not one answer.** `Access::effective('chat')` applies no hook of its own, so `overridden('chat')` is always false. Task 21 asks per surface instead: `Capability::filtered('alpaca_bot/admin/menu_capability', $stored)` for the screen, and the route hook for the API, and says which surface a filter changed.
5. **Never call `effective()`/`overridden()` with no arguments for a row whose filter takes a second parameter.** A listener registered with `accepted_args` 2 throws `ArgumentCountError` when the hook fires with one. Task 21 passes the same shape the runtime passes, inside a try/catch so a site's filter cannot fatal the settings page. Tasks 8, 10, 22 and 27 each state what their row's filter receives; reconcile Task 21's `accessArgs()` arms against them as they land.
6. **The drawer gate is `Menu::capability()`, not `Access::allows($userId, 'chat')`.** `alpaca_bot/capability/chat` is `POST /chat`'s route filter and its documented signature takes a `WP_REST_Request` (`docs/api.md:123-126`); calling it from `admin_footer` with no request would hand a two-argument listener one argument. Tasks 18 and 20 use the menu capability, which is the screen's question anyway.
7. **`HooksDocTest` pins the hook set and its count in the test name** (22 today, 23 after Task 6, 24 after Task 9). Every later task that adds a hook — Task 27's `alpaca_bot/mcp/called` among them — re-counts rather than assuming. Run `composer docs:hooks` and that test together.
8. **Tasks 8 and 13 rewrite the same paragraphs** in `src/Rest/Controller.php:76-83`, `bin/hooks-doc.php:535-542` and `src/Admin/HelpTabs.php:120-128`. Task 8 goes first and rewrites the "only capability gate over the tools" argument; Task 13 then changes only the egress-policy and rebinding clauses, applied to the text as Task 8 left it.
9. **`symfony/http-client` is prefixed today only because php-agents requires it.** It is not in `composer.json` `extra.strauss.packages`, so `Mcp\Egress` would depend on a prefixed package nothing declares. **Task 14 adds `symfony/http-client` to that list** as its first step, and Task 28 re-checks it after the php-agents bump.
10. **The Access tab exists from Task 6, not Task 21.** `SettingsPage` renders a tab per schema section, so the section arrives with working selects. Task 21 is a redesign of a live tab: the "set in code" line, the capability labels and the MCP rows.
11. **Task 5 and Task 16 share the markers.** If Task 5 leaves `OPEN`/`CLOSE` as the literals at `Pipeline.php:672` and `:745`, Task 16 adds the constants and rewrites those two uses.
12. **Tasks 1, 3 and 5 all edit `Pipeline.php`**, so run them in number order. Every line number in this plan is as of `2949f14`.
13. **`Registry::register()` takes an optional row** (`?string $row = null`) from Task 8, so Task 27 can register MCP toolkits under `mcp.<id>` without `enabled()` having to infer the prefix.
14. **Task 27's receipt change ripples into existing assertions:** `AgentStreamObserverTest` (:50, :59, :112) and `UsageMeterTest` (:33) pin exact arrays that gain `result_bytes`/`tool_result_bytes`.

## Decisions taken while drafting (confirm at review)

1. **Kanboard #4322 is nearly closed already.** Commit `d46a096` (2026-09-09) removed the `[#2977]` probes and showed that the `ollama.internal` line was fixture text, not a DNS lookup. Task 2 is now the remaining gap: a test that doesn't stub the provider would still quietly dial the configured base URL, so the bootstrap gains an offline guard that fails loudly.
2. **The spec's `TerminationException('')` case is stale.** The #4311 review dropped the guard that made it a failure, and `PipelineToolsTest.php:1079` pins it as a normal stop. The one path that still takes the prefix is a toolkit throwing from `tools()`, which Task 3 handles.
3. **Suppression marks rather than withholds (Task 16).** Every byte still ships, flagged `held` on the delta frame, so `Pipeline`'s `$content`, `recovered()` and the "deltas concatenate to the reply" contract survive, and a client that ignores the flag behaves exactly as 0.5 does. The spec's "buffer only from the opening marker" described the client's behaviour; this is where the knowledge lives.
4. **Kanboard #4333's ticket note is wrong** and Task 11 corrects it on the board: the redemption is a `fetch()`, not an `EventSource` (`stream.ts:2` says why), and `chat.ts:146` already reads the error body. What's missing is only the distinct code and the sentence naming which limit was hit.
5. **`alpaca-bot/*` abilities keep their hardcoded `edit_posts`** rather than following the Chat row. They are a different surface (MCP clients, WP-CLI, other plugins) with their own permission callbacks, and `Register`'s docblock argues against a second filter seam. Task 23 writes that reasoning into the docblock so the next reader doesn't have to guess.
6. **`alpaca_bot/capability/settings/schema` is retired** by Task 9 — the one back-compat break in a phase that otherwise breaks nothing. Task 29's upgrade notice carries it beside the tool floor.
7. **MCP egress ignores environment proxies** (`no_proxy: '*'`), so the pin can't go inert through a proxy. The cost: a host with a mandatory outbound proxy cannot reach MCP servers in 0.6. web_fetch, by contrast, still honours `WP_PROXY_*` and discloses that the pin stops there. Tasks 25 and 29 say so.
8. **web_fetch's own-host exemption stays as it is** (neither looked up nor pinned). `Egress` has no exemption, so a site-local MCP server needs core's `http_request_host_is_external` opt-in.
9. **`Mcp\FakeClient` ships in `src/`.** It is inert without a caller that hands `ClientFactory` a build closure, and the integration suite loads the plugin the way WordPress does, which is why it isn't under `tests/`.
10. **`happy-dom` is the candidate for Task 15**, at whatever version the supply-chain vet clears. `linkedom` was rejected because `boot()` needs `getComputedStyle`, `form.elements.namedItem` and `navigator.onLine`. If the vet fails, stop and ask Carmelo; switching to `linkedom` costs one ported test.
11. **The drawer's budget is measured by `get_num_queries()` in an integration test**, not Query Monitor: the 0.5 baseline's mu-plugin probe recorded that same counter, and QM is not verifiably installed on alpaca10 any more.
12. **`Mcp\McpToolkit` lists a server's tools once per turn** (memoised per instance). That is what makes a changed definition take effect immediately, at the cost of one HTTP round trip per server per turn.
13. **Tool names are fitted to 64 characters** of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` with a letter or `_` first (`Toolkit\ToolName::fit()`), hash-marked when a name has to change. That is OpenAI's limit; other providers' limits were not each verified.
14. **`provider.api_key` is masked but autoloaded today** (`Store::replace()` and `register_setting()` pass no `$autoload`, so the whole option sits in `alloptions`). MCP header values therefore go in a separate non-autoloaded option rather than following the provider key's example. Moving the provider key itself is a migration, filed separately, not release work.

## Known gaps

- **The abandoned-turn leak is still not recovered** (Kanboard #4329 comment 617 §2): `settle()` → `storePartial()` never passes through `FakedToolCall::recovered()`, so an abandoned tool turn stores raw markup. Task 16 makes the two paths agree about which bytes are stored, and flags the recovery itself as remaining work.
- **Task 23 has no failing test.** Its claims are about WordPress core at two tags; core isn't in the repo, the unit suite stubs WordPress, and fetching core would break Task 2's hermetic rule. The gate is the stated verification commands.
- **Kanboard #4335's bound is per batch.** The loop only occurs with 100 or more unsaveable rows; a fatal inside the loop is still unbounded, because the count is written after it.
- **The original P5 fuzz harness can't be recovered** (its ledger was gitignored in a removed worktree). Task 5 rebuilds it as a seeded differential run: capture outputs before the move, compare after.
- **One transport hole in Task 13:** a `requests-requests.before_request` listener can set the transport by reference after the pre-check. The fail-closed `CurlPin` throw covers the request itself.
